# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# STAGED — NOT APPLIED TO THE LIVE DECK.
#
# This file is a curation decision UNDER REVIEW. tools/build-set.js reads it to
# work out what a filtered printing WOULD look like, and ships nothing on the
# strength of it. The live deck is built from catalog/excluded.txt alone.
#
# To apply it once review is finished:
#     node tools/build-set.js --apply-staged     (or npm run deploy:filtered)
#
# WHY THIS IS A SEPARATE FILE, and it is a mistake being corrected rather than a
# feature. These 8,430 ids were appended straight into catalog/excluded.txt,
# which is the SETTLED list the build always applies — so an in-progress
# judgement went live the moment it was written, and 42% of the deck disappeared
# from a public site before anybody had reviewed a line of it. A pending decision
# must not be able to reach production by default. Two files, two states:
#
#     catalog/excluded.txt                settled — always applied
#     catalog/excluded-institutions.txt   staged  — applied on request
#
# Nothing here is deleted or at risk: every id is still in catalog/candidates.json
# and `npm run status` fails if that ever stops being true. Marking review
# decisions happens in reports/review-queue.txt; tools/reinstate.js deletes ids
# from whichever file holds them.
# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# INSTITUTIONS AND B2B BRANDS  (added 2026-08-03, generated — see below)
#
# THE RULE, and it is a risk rule rather than a taste one:
#   A PERSON gets a card. AN INSTITUTION DOES NOT.
#
# Ash's framing, which is better than the one this file opened with: a
# creator like KSI has no reason to mind being on a card, while a company,
# university or trade association has a trademark budget, a legal team and a
# policy about its marks. The downside is asymmetric — one side sends a
# thank-you and the other sends a letter — and there are far more
# personalities available than slots to put them in. So the institutions go,
# and nothing of value is lost.
#
# This is NOT the opt-out denylist (catalog/denylist.json), which records
# creators who ASKED to be removed and is permanent. These entries are
# editorial and revisable, exactly like the ones above.
#
# HOW THE LINE WAS DRAWN — structurally, not by reading 19,874 names.
# Each channel was looked up on Wikidata by its channel id (P2397) and
# classified on P31, "instance of". That asks what a thing IS rather than
# what it is called, so it does not care that "Traversy Media" is one person
# and "Rexam Plc" is not.
#
#   CUT     business · public company · nonprofit · university · school
#           district · government agency · museum · record label · think
#           tank · advocacy group · football club · TV series/channel ·
#           video game and its developer · brand · website · publisher
#   KEPT    human · musical group, rock band, duo · comedy troupe ·
#           podcast · YouTube channel
#
# A BAND IS A PERFORMER. Arctic Monkeys and The White Stripes stay for the
# same reason KSI does; their RECORD LABEL goes. That distinction is the
# whole rule in one line.
#
# The 2,238 cards with no Wikidata item at all were KEPT. They are the
# sub-100K commons that keyword search found — individuals who simply have
# no encyclopedia article. A narrow name screen (Inc, LLC, Ltd, Plc, GmbH,
# University, Institute, Foundation, Association, Records, ...) was applied
# to them and caught a further handful. "Media", "Studios", "Network" and
# "Group" were deliberately LEFT OUT of that screen: they are as common in a
# solo creator's channel name as in a corporation's.
#
# COST, stated plainly: 8,379 of 19,874 cards, 42% of the printing.
# N 6735 · R 1137 · SR 421 · SSR 84 · UR 2. The deck lands at 11,495. The N
# band takes almost all of it, which makes sense — an encyclopedia has an
# article for every school district in America and for very few small
# creators.
#
# GENERATED, and the honest consequence is that some of these are wrong.
# Wikidata says Beast Philanthropy is a 501(c)(3), which it is, though nobody
# imagines MrBeast objecting. Deleting a line here puts a card back.
# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

# ── The tie-break that let 72 through, hand-read (2026-08-03) ──────────────
#
# The generated pass above used "performer wins" when Wikidata gave a channel
# BOTH a performer type and an organisation type. YouTube itself survived on
# that rule: its P31 list contains "YouTube channel" alongside "public
# company". A tag saying what a thing is ON cannot outrank one saying what it
# IS.
#
# But a blanket "organisation wins" is just as wrong, and the 72 show why:
# Sidemen, NELK, GameGrumps, h3h3Productions, Yes Theory, OfflineTV and
# Kurzgesagt are all filed as "organization", "business" or "company" —
# because creator collectives incorporate. Sidemen is KSI, the exact person
# the rule was written to keep.
#
# So neither tie-break is right and 72 is small enough to READ, which is the
# standard this project already uses when a band is small enough to check.
# 51 cut, 21 kept. The kept ones are creator collectives that happen to be
# incorporated; the cut ones are platforms (every Crunchyroll locale, Prime
# Video, Disney Plus, discovery plus), labels, brands, orchestras and NGOs.
#
# THE LESSON, since it generalises: when two structural signals disagree, the
# tie-break is a JUDGEMENT and cannot be resolved by preferring one field.
# Surface the disagreements and read them — there are never many.
